入库实录逐步对照字段首次规划实际入库后续目录逐步回放与原文对照 →
任务与分工　job 是一张卡的写作任务。Agent 可以自行处理，也可把独立任务交给子 Agent（worker）。本例按 jobId 跟踪实际调用。

从上一步产出追到下一次调用

并排核对同名字段、重命名字段及 Agent 新增的内容。每段均可展开相应事件的原始输入与回包。

1. 来源名称从登记响应传给启动请求2. 启动快照与主题计划3. 同主题在新会话重新规划4. 续读句柄从程序回包传回同一计划5. 确认计划并把任务交给处理该卡的 Agent6. 任务回执授权读哪一份原文7. Agent 根据原文组织主张并关联证据8. 接收主张并记录持久标识9. 通过版本找回正文，通过查询核对身份

01　Agent 选择来源名称，程序确认已登记

来源名称从登记响应传给启动请求

首次登记与重新登记各有自己的启动。两个 sourceId 和两个 sessionId 分别保留。

05　程序绑定 jobId 和 sourceReads

确认计划并把任务交给处理该卡的 Agent

PLANNED 回包已含 NUMA 的任务及标识。同一 sessionId 传给 prepare 后，程序确认任务并再次返回同一组 jobId、knowledgeId、sourceReads。此处 job 是 NUMA 卡的写作任务。Agent 取得任务后可自行处理，也可分给子 Agent（worker）；日志记录了实际使用的 jobId。

上一步的产出

  "sessionId": "8a2a655b-5f25-4e8a-ae46-d241f53c1123",

      "3bcb154e3cc56e3482fd24072b09edd93cfebec6aec0d0c37abcfbbd1a3c7ce3"

06　Agent 从分配的任务取三个参数

任务回执授权读哪一份原文

jobId 取自 job；documentId 和 receiptId 取自它的 sourceReads[0]。读回的 receipt.id 再次确认同一回执，content 是 Agent 据以归纳的原文。

上一步的产出

{
  "jobId": "54138402-54d3-40ac-9d75-f841bab85203",
  "sourceReads": [
    {
      "documentId": "v2-01-numa",
      "receiptId": "fc7b069d-780f-4fec-99fa-7805f62bf31c",
      "startLine": 1,
      "endLine": 294
    }
  ]
}

Based on the features of the Kunpeng 920 processor, this document describes how to optimize code performance during Kunpeng application development from multiple dimensions.\n\nFirst, the Kunpeng processor is briefly introduced. The Kunpeng processor is an ARM-based enterprise-level processor. In terms of the general-purpose computing processor, Huawei released the first ARM-based 64-bit Kunpeng 912 processor in 2014, and the Kunpeng 916 processor released in 2016 is the industry's first ARM-based processor that supports multi-socket interconnection. The third-generation Kunpeng 920 processor released in January 2019 is the industry's first ARM-based processor at data center level that uses the 7 nm process.\n\nFigure 1 shows the evolution roadmap of the Huawei Kunpeng processor family.\n\n**Figure 1** Kunpeng processor roadmap\n\n<!-- ASCII Diagram: Kunpeng 处理器发展路线图 -->\n```\nCores\n  │\n64┤                                          ┌──────────────┐\n  │                                          │  Kunpeng 920 │  • 24/32/48/64 x Huawei-developed core V1\n  │                                          └──────────────┘  • Up to 3 GHz\n  │                                                            • 4P, 8 x 64-bit DDR4\n  │                                                            • RoCEv2  • PCIe 4.0/100GE\n40┤\n  │    • 32 x A57@16FF                                ┌───────────────┐\n  │    • 2.1 GHz                                      │  Kunpeng 920S │  • 24/32 x Huawei-developed core V1\n32┤    • 4 x 64-bit DDR3/4   ┌──────────────┐         └───────────────┘  • Up to 3 GHz\n  │  ┌──────────────┐        │  Kunpeng 916 │                            • 2P, 4 x 64-bit DDR3/4\n  │  │  Kunpeng 912 │        └──────────────┘                            • RoCEv2  • PCIe 4.0/100GE\n  │  └──────────────┘        • 32 x A57@16FF+\n  │                          • 2.4 GHz, 2P interconnection\n  │                          • 4 x 64-bit DDR3/4\n16┤  ┌──────────────┐        • PCIe 3.0/SAS 3.0/10GE\n  │  │  Kunpeng 910 │\n  │  └──────────────┘\n  │  • 16 x A57@16FF\n  │  • 2.1 GHz\n  │  • 2 x 64-bit DDR3/4\n  │  • PCIe 3.0/SAS 3.0/10GE\n  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────► Year\n              2015            2017            2019\n```\n\n\nThis document describes the Kunpeng programming optimization methods in terms of hardware and software. Kunpeng processor.\n\nThe Kunpeng 920 processor uses the non-uniform memory access (NUMA) architecture, which solves the restriction of the symmetrical multiprocessing (SMP) technology on the number of CPU cores. Therefore, multi-core is one of major advantages of the Kunpeng processor. However, when programs run concurrently, cross-NUMA memory access hinders program performance. This document describes some programming methods for improving NUMA affinity of programs in [Multi-core and NUMA](<kunpengprogramming_05_0002.html>).\n\nThe SoC of the Kunpeng 920 processor uses the core developed by Huawei HiSilicon. For a single processor core, the instruction time parallel technology, that is, instruction pipeline, plays a leading role in performance improvement. The Kunpeng 920 processor supports multi-level pipelines. [Pipeline](<kunpengprogramming_05_0006.html>) describes how to optimize pipeline orchestration to improve the throughput and efficiency of the pipeline and give full play to the processor performance. In [Cache and Prefetch](<kunpengprogramming_05_0012.html>), based on the L3 cache of the Kunpeng 920 processor, this document introduces some program data structure arrangement and access methods, which can significantly improve the cache hit ratio.\n\nAt the software level, instruction sets, compilers, and acceleration libraries are introduced.\n\nThe Kunpeng 920 processor is fully compatible with the Armv8-A instruction set. For details about how to use the NEON instructions, see [SIMD Programming](<kunpengprogramming_05_0016.html>). After the code development is complete, the compiler needs to translate the code into executable files that adapt to different platforms. This document describes how to use the features of the compiler to optimize code in [Compiler](<kunpengprogramming_05_0029.html>).\n\nIn addition to the preceding common methods, Huawei releases the Kunpeng BoostKit, a full-stack optimization solution for Kunpeng hardware, base software, and application software. It provides high-performance open source components, basic acceleration software packages, and application acceleration software packages to enable optimal application performance. In [Kunpeng BoostKit Library](<kunpengprogramming_05_0034.html>), this document describes how to use the Kunpeng basic acceleration libraries.\n\n---\ntitle: \"Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP\"\nsource: \"https://www.hikunpeng.com/document/detail/en/perftuning/progtuneg/kunpengprogramming_05_0002.html\"\ncrawled_at: \"2026-04-18 14:57:43\"\n---\n\n# Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP\n\nIn the early stage of computer science, most computers were equipped with a chip on the mainboard, which is called a microprocessor or a single-core CPU. These processors communicate with other components on the mainboard through a connector or socket. It is very inefficient for processors to communicate with each other by using a system bus. A performance bottleneck often occurs, and the computing capability of a CPU cannot be maximized.\n\nTo deal with it, the hyper-threading technology is developed. The hyper-threading technology copies CPU units (such as registers or the L1 cache) to the same processor, so that two execution threads can share data. This mode accelerates multi-process execution and provides higher overall performance than conventional single-core mode (hyper-threading disabled).\n\nWith the rapid development of informatization and intelligence in modern society, more and more devices are connected to the Internet, Internet of Things (IoT), and Internet of Vehicles (IoV), resulting in huge computing requirements. The multi-core architecture is an inevitable trend. The symmetric multi-processing (SMP) technology uses the symmetric multi-processor structure. Each processor is equal and has the same permissions to use the memory. Any program, process, or thread can be allocated to any processor. With the support of the OS, perfect load balancing can be achieved, which greatly improves the performance and throughput of the entire system. However, because multiple cores use a same bus to access the memory, as the core number increases, memory access conflicts increase rapidly, and the bus becomes a bottleneck, which restricts scalability and performance of the system.\n\n**Figure 1** SMP architecture\n\n<!-- ASCII Diagram: UMA 统一内存访问架构（单 Memory Controller） -->\n```\n          ┌───────┐  ┌───────┐  ┌───────┐  ┌───────┐\n          │ Core1 │  │ Core2 │  │ Core3 │  │ Core4 │\n          └───┬───┘  └───┬───┘  └───┬───┘  └───┬───┘\n              └──────────┴──────────┴──────────┘\n                                   │\n              ┌────────┐  ┌────────┴────────┐  ┌────────┐\n              │ [RAM]  │  │                 │  │ [RAM]  │\n              │ [RAM]  ├──┤ Memory          ├──┤ [RAM]  │\n              └────────┘  │ Controller      │  └────────┘\n                          └─────────────────┘\n```\n\n\n**Parent topic:** [Multi-core and NUMA](<kunpengprogramming_05_0002.html>)\n---\ntitle: \"Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP\"\nsource: \"https://www.hikunpeng.com/document/detail/en/perftuning/progtuneg/kunpengprogramming_05_0003.html\"\ncrawled_at: \"2026-04-18 14:57:44\"\nredirect_to: \"./document_detail_en_perftuning_progtuneg_kunpengprogramming_05_0002.md\"\n---\n\n# Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP\n\n> ℹ️ This page is a duplicate of [Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP](./document_detail_en_perftuning_progtuneg_kunpengprogramming_05_0002.md) captured by the original\n> crawler (parent + child topic share the same body). The full\n> content, including ASCII diagrams, lives in the canonical page.\n\n---\ntitle: \"NUMA Structure\"\nsource: \"https://www.hikunpeng.com/document/detail/en/perftuning/progtuneg/kunpengprogramming_05_0004.html\"\ncrawled_at: \"2026-04-18 14:57:45\"\n---\n\n# NUMA Structure\n\nThe SMP centralized shared memory limits the memory access frequency of the processor. Therefore, the processor may be frequently in data access starvation. To better solve this problem, the NUMA architecture is developed.\n\nIn the NUMA architecture, multiple cores are bound into a node, and each node could be considered as a symmetric multiprocessor. Nodes of a CPU communicate with each other through the on-chip network, and different CPUs communicate with each other through Hydra interfaces with high bandwidth and low latency. In the NUMA architecture, the entire memory space is physically distributed, and a set of all these dual in-line memory modules (DIMMs) is the global memory of the entire system. The memory access time of each core depends on the location of the memory relative to the processor. The access to the local memory (on the local node) is faster. The Linux kernel supports the NUMA architecture since version 2.5. The current OSs also provide various tools and interfaces to help optimize and configure the nearest memory access. The Kunpeng processor supports the NUMA architecture. Therefore, a computer system implemented by using the Kunpeng processor can achieve good performance and resolve bus bottlenecks in the SMP architecture through performance tuning, providing stronger multi-core scalability and better and more flexible computing capability.\n\n**Figure 1** NUMA architecture\n\n<!-- ASCII Diagram: 双 CPU NUMA 架构拓扑图 -->\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────┐   ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐\n│               CPU 1                 │   │               CPU 2                 │\n│                                     │   │                                     │\n│  ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │   │  ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │\n│  │        NUMA Node 1           │   │   │  │        NUMA Node 1           │   │\n│  │  ┌────────────┐ │  │   │   │  ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │\n│  │  └────────────┘  └────────┘  │   │   │  │        NUMA Node 2           │   │\n│  └──────────────────────────────┘   │   │  │  ┌────────────┐            Memory Controller  │   │\n│  │  │ Controller │  ├────────┤  │   │   │  └──────────────────────────────┘   │\n│  │  │            │  │ Core5  │  │   │   │ Use](</legal/law>)\n---\ntitle: \"Affinity and Core Binding\"\nsource: \"https://www.hikunpeng.com/document/detail/en/perftuning/progtuneg/kunpengprogramming_05_0005.html\"\ncrawled_at: \"2026-04-18 14:57:46\"\n---\n\n# Affinity and Core Binding\n\nAffinity is the tendency of a process to run on a specified CPU as long as possible without being scheduled to other processors. On a multi-core server, each CPU has its own cache that stores the information used by processes. A process may be scheduled by the OS to other CPUs. As a result, the CPU cache hit ratio is low. After the CPU is bound, the process keeps running on the specified CPU, and the OS does not schedule the process to other CPUs. In this way, the CPU cache hit ratio is greatly improved, and thus the performance is improved.\n\n1. Run the **shell** command to bind the running task to a NUMA node and CPU.\n\nThe **numactl** command is a manual optimization command provided by Linux. It can be used to specify a process to run on a NUMA node or a specific CPU core.\n\n1. Bind a NUMA node: **numactl --cpubind=0 --membind=0 java SIMDTest_Compare_Max2**\n2. Bind a CPU core: **numactl -C 0-19 --membind=0 java SIMDTest_Compare_Max2**\n3. Run the **top** command to check whether CPU core binding is successful. The output also shows the process to which the core is bound.\n2. Specify a CPU core by calling the system APIs in the program code.\n\nThe **sched_getaffinity** interface in the glibc library is used to obtain the current CPU affinity of the application. The **sched_setaffinity** interface can be used to bind the application to one or more CPU cores.\n\nThe API syntax is as follows:\n[code]#include <sched.h>\nint sched_setaffinity(pid_t pid, unsigned int cpusetsize, cpu_set_t *mask);\nint sched_getaffinity(pid_t pid, unsigned int cpusetsize, cpu_set_t *mask);\nvoid CPU_CLR(int cpu, cpu_set_t *set);\nint CPU_ISSET(int cpu, cpu_set_t *set);\nvoid CPU_SET(int cpu, cpu_set_t *set);\nvoid CPU_ZERO(cpu_set_t *set);\n[/code]\n\nThe following is an example of core binding:\n[code] #include <sched.h>\ncpu_set_t cpu_mask;\nmemset((VOS_VOID *)(&cpu_mask), 0 , sizeof(cpu_mask));\ncpu_mask.__bits[0] = 1 << 0;\n(VOS_VOID)sched_setaffinity(0, sizeof(cpu_mask), &cpu_mask);\n[/code]\n\n3. Bind application software to cores.\n\nThe Kunpeng 920 processor provides two super CPU clusters (SCCLs). Each SCCL contains six to eight CPU clusters, and each CPU cluster contains four cores. When binding CPUs to a KVM, you are advised to use CPUs across multiple CPU clusters to improve the KVM performance. This method can reduce bandwidth bottlenecks between the L3 cache and memory caused by core contention in the same CPU cluster.\n\n1. Query the NUMA node information and topology in the Linux system.\n[code]numactl -H\n[/code]\n\n2. Edit the VM XML configuration file in the Linux system and bind vCPUs to cores in as many CPU clusters as possible. The following is an example:\n[code]<domain type = 'KVM'>\n...\n<vcpu placement = 'static' cpuset='0,1,4,5,8,9,12,13'>8</vcpu>\n<cputune>\n<vcpupin vcpu='0' cpuset='0'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='1' cpuset='1'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='2' cpuset='4'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='3' cpuset='5'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='4' cpuset='8'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='5' cpuset='9'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='6' cpuset='12'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='7' cpuset='13'/>\n</cputune>\n...\n</domain>\n[/code]\n\n(0, 1), (4, 5), (8, 9), and (12, 13) are in different CPU clusters.\n\n**Parent topic:** [Multi-core and NUMA](<kunpengprogramming_05_0002.html>)\n",

Kunpeng processor.\n\nThe Kunpeng 920 processor uses the non-uniform memory access (NUMA) architecture, which solves the restriction of the symmetrical multiprocessing (SMP) technology on the number of CPU cores. Therefore, multi-core is one of major advantages of the Kunpeng processor. However, when programs run concurrently, cross-NUMA memory access hinders program performance. This document describes some programming methods for improving NUMA affinity of programs in [Multi-core and NUMA](<kunpengprogramming_05_0002.html>).\n\nThe SoC of the Kunpeng 920 processor uses the core developed by Huawei HiSilicon. For a single processor core, the instruction time parallel technology, that is, instruction pipeline, plays a leading role in performance improvement. The Kunpeng 920 processor supports multi-level pipelines. [Pipeline](<kunpengprogramming_05_0006.html>) describes how to optimize pipeline orchestration to improve the throughput and efficiency of the pipeline and give full play to the processor performance. For details about how to use the NEON instructions, see [SIMD Programming](<kunpengprogramming_05_0016.html>). After the code development is complete, the compiler needs to translate the code into executable files that adapt to different platforms. This document describes how to use the features of the compiler to optimize code in [Compiler](<kunpengprogramming_05_0029.html>).\n\nIn addition to the preceding common methods, Huawei releases the Kunpeng BoostKit, a full-stack optimization solution for Kunpeng hardware, base software, and application software. It provides high-performance open source components, basic acceleration software packages, and application acceleration software packages to enable optimal application performance. In [Kunpeng BoostKit Library](<kunpengprogramming_05_0034.html>), this document describes how to use the Kunpeng basic acceleration libraries.\n\n---\ntitle: \"Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP\"\nsource: \"https://www.hikunpeng.com/document/detail/en/perftuning/progtuneg/kunpengprogramming_05_0002.html\"\ncrawled_at: \"2026-04-18 14:57:43\"\n---\n\n# Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP\n\nIn the early stage of computer science, most computers were equipped with a chip on the mainboard, which is called a microprocessor or a single-core CPU. These processors communicate with other components on the mainboard through a connector or socket. It is very inefficient for processors to communicate with each other by using a system bus. A performance bottleneck often occurs, and the computing capability of a CPU cannot be maximized.\n\nTo deal with it, the hyper-threading technology is developed. The hyper-threading technology copies CPU units (such as registers or the L1 cache) to the same processor, so that two execution threads can share data. This mode accelerates multi-process execution and provides higher overall performance than conventional single-core mode (hyper-threading disabled).\n\nWith the rapid development of informatization and intelligence in modern society, more and more devices are connected to the Internet, Internet of Things (IoT), and Internet of Vehicles (IoV), resulting in huge computing requirements. The multi-core architecture is an inevitable trend. The symmetric multi-processing (SMP) technology uses the symmetric multi-processor structure. Each processor is equal and has the same permissions to use the memory. Any program, process, or thread can be allocated to any processor. With the support of the OS, perfect load balancing can be achieved, which greatly improves the performance and throughput of the entire system. However, because multiple cores use a same bus to access the memory, as the core number increases, memory access conflicts increase rapidly, and the bus becomes a bottleneck, which restricts scalability and performance of the system.\n\n**Figure 1** SMP architecture\n\n<!-- ASCII Diagram: UMA 统一内存访问架构（单 Memory Controller） -->\n```\n          ┌───────┐  ┌───────┐  ┌───────┐  ┌───────┐\n          │ Core1 │  │ Core2 │  │ Core3 │  │ Core4 │\n          └───┬───┘  └───┬───┘  └───┬───┘  └───┬───┘\n              └──────────┴──────────┴──────────┘\n                                   │\n              ┌────────┐  ┌────────┴────────┐  ┌────────┐\n              │ [RAM]  │  │                 │  │ [RAM]  │\n              │ [RAM]  ├──┤ Memory          ├──┤ [RAM]  │\n              └────────┘  │ Controller      │  └────────┘\n                          └─────────────────┘\n```\n\n\n**Parent topic:** [Multi-core and NUMA](<kunpengprogramming_05_0002.html>)\n---\ntitle: \"Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP\"\nsource: \"https://www.hikunpeng.com/document/detail/en/perftuning/progtuneg/kunpengprogramming_05_0003.html\"\ncrawled_at: \"2026-04-18 14:57:44\"\nredirect_to: \"./document_detail_en_perftuning_progtuneg_kunpengprogramming_05_0002.md\"\n---\n\n# Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP\n\n> ℹ️ This page is a duplicate of [Single-core CPU, Hyper-threading, and SMP](./document_detail_en_perftuning_progtuneg_kunpengprogramming_05_0002.md) captured by the original\n> crawler (parent + child topic share the same body). The full\n> content, including ASCII diagrams, lives in the canonical page.\n\n---\ntitle: \"NUMA Structure\"\nsource: \"https://www.hikunpeng.com/document/detail/en/perftuning/progtuneg/kunpengprogramming_05_0004.html\"\ncrawled_at: \"2026-04-18 14:57:45\"\n---\n\n# NUMA Structure\n\nThe SMP centralized shared memory limits the memory access frequency of the processor. Therefore, the processor may be frequently in data access starvation. To better solve this problem, the NUMA architecture is developed.\n\nIn the NUMA architecture, multiple cores are bound into a node, and each node could be considered as a symmetric multiprocessor. Nodes of a CPU communicate with each other through the on-chip network, and different CPUs communicate with each other through Hydra interfaces with high bandwidth and low latency. In the NUMA architecture, the entire memory space is physically distributed, and a set of all these dual in-line memory modules (DIMMs) is the global memory of the entire system. The memory access time of each core depends on the location of the memory relative to the processor. The access to the local memory (on the local node) is faster. The Linux kernel supports the NUMA architecture since version 2.5. The current OSs also provide various tools and interfaces to help optimize and configure the nearest memory access. The Kunpeng processor supports the NUMA architecture. Therefore, a computer system implemented by using the Kunpeng processor can achieve good performance and resolve bus bottlenecks in the SMP architecture through performance tuning, providing stronger multi-core scalability and better and more flexible computing capability.\n\n**Figure 1** NUMA architecture\n\n<!-- ASCII Diagram: 双 CPU NUMA 架构拓扑图 -->\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────┐   ┌─────────────────────────────────────┐\n│               CPU 1                 │   │               CPU 2                 │\n│                                     │   │                                     │\n│  ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │   │  ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │\n│  │        NUMA Node 1           │   │   │  │        NUMA Node 1           │   │\n│  │  ┌────────────┐  ┌────────┐  │   │   │  │  ┌────────────┐  ┌────────┐  │   │\n│  │  │   Memory   │  │ Core0  │  │   │   │  │  │ Core0      │  │ Core1  │  │   │\n│  │  │ Controller │  ├────────┤  │   │   │  │  ├────────────┤  ├────────┤  │   │\n│  │  │            │  │ Core1  │  │   │   │  │  │ Core2      │  │ Core3  │  │   │\n│  │  │            │  ├────────┤  │   │   │  │  └────────────┘  └────────┘  │   │\n│  │  │            │  │ Core2  │  │   │   │  └──────────────────────────────┘   │\n│  │  │            │  ├────────┤  │   │   │                                     │\n│  │  │            │  │ Core3  │  │   │   │  ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │\n│  │  └────────────┘  └────────┘  │   │   │  │        NUMA Node 2           │   │\n│  └──────────────────────────────┘   │   │  │  ┌────────────┐  ┌────────┐  │   │\n│                                     │   │  │  │ Core4      │  │ Core5  │  │   │\n│  ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │   │  │  ├────────────┤  ├────────┤  │   │\n│  │        NUMA Node 2           │   │   │  │  │ Core6      │  │ Core7  │  │   │\n│  │  ┌────────────┐  ┌────────┐  │   │   │  │  └────────────┘  └────────┘  │   │\n│  │  │   Memory   │  │ Core4  │  │   │   │  │            Memory Controller  │   │\n│  │  │ Controller │  ├────────┤  │   │   │  └──────────────────────────────┘   │\n│  │  │            │  │ Core5  │  │   │   │ NUMA2\n│  │  │            │  │ Core7  │  │    [RAM]──CPU2 NUMA1    [RAM]──CPU2 NUMA2\n│  │  └────────────┘  └────────┘  │\n│  └──────────────────────────────┘\n└─────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n\n\n<!-- ASCII Diagram: numactl -H 输出（NUMA 节点信息） -->\n```\n(base) [root@hpc42 ~]# numactl -H\n\navailable: 4 nodes          108 109 110 111 112 113 114 115 116 117 118 119\n             120 121 125 126 127\nnode 3 size:  64426 MB\nnode 3 free:  61653 MB\n\nnode distances:\nnode    0    1    2    3\n  0:   10   16 Use](</legal/law>)\n---\ntitle: \"Affinity and Core Binding\"\nsource: \"https://www.hikunpeng.com/document/detail/en/perftuning/progtuneg/kunpengprogramming_05_0005.html\"\ncrawled_at: \"2026-04-18 14:57:46\"\n---\n\n# Affinity and Core Binding\n\nAffinity is the tendency of a process to run on a specified CPU as long as possible without being scheduled to other processors. On a multi-core server, each CPU has its own cache that stores the information used by processes. A process may be scheduled by the OS to other CPUs. As a result, the CPU cache hit ratio is low. After the CPU is bound, the process keeps running on the specified CPU, and the OS does not schedule the process to other CPUs. In this way, the CPU cache hit ratio is greatly improved, and thus the performance is improved.\n\n1. Run the **shell** command to bind the running task to a NUMA node and CPU.\n\nThe **numactl** command is a manual optimization command provided by Linux. It can be used to specify a process to run on a NUMA node or a specific CPU core.\n\n1. Bind a NUMA node: **numactl --cpubind=0 --membind=0 java SIMDTest_Compare_Max2**\n2. Bind a CPU core: **numactl -C 0-19 --membind=0 java SIMDTest_Compare_Max2**\n3. Run the **top** command to check whether CPU core binding is successful. The output also shows the process to which the core is bound.\n2. Specify a CPU core by calling the system APIs in the program code.\n\nThe **sched_getaffinity** interface in the glibc library is used to obtain the current CPU affinity of the application. The **sched_setaffinity** interface can be used to bind the application to one or more CPU cores.\n\nThe API syntax is as follows:\n[code]#include <sched.h>\nint sched_setaffinity(pid_t pid, unsigned int cpusetsize, cpu_set_t *mask);\nint sched_getaffinity(pid_t pid, unsigned int cpusetsize, cpu_set_t *mask);\nvoid CPU_CLR(int cpu, cpu_set_t *set);\nint CPU_ISSET(int cpu, cpu_set_t *set);\nvoid CPU_SET(int cpu, cpu_set_t *set);\nvoid CPU_ZERO(cpu_set_t *set);\n[/code]\n\nThe following is an example of core binding:\n[code] #include <sched.h>\ncpu_set_t cpu_mask;\nmemset((VOS_VOID *)(&cpu_mask), 0 , sizeof(cpu_mask));\ncpu_mask.__bits[0] = 1 << 0;\n(VOS_VOID)sched_setaffinity(0, sizeof(cpu_mask), &cpu_mask);\n[/code]\n\n3. Bind application software to cores.\n\nThe Kunpeng 920 processor provides two super CPU clusters (SCCLs). Each SCCL contains six to eight CPU clusters, and each CPU cluster contains four cores. When binding CPUs to a KVM, you are advised to use CPUs across multiple CPU clusters to improve the KVM performance. This method can reduce bandwidth bottlenecks between the L3 cache and memory caused by core contention in the same CPU cluster.\n\n1. Query the NUMA node information and topology in the Linux system.\n[code]numactl -H\n[/code]\n\n2. Edit the VM XML configuration file in the Linux system and bind vCPUs to cores in as many CPU clusters as possible. The following is an example:\n[code]<domain type = 'KVM'>\n...\n<vcpu placement = 'static' cpuset='0,1,4,5,8,9,12,13'>8</vcpu>\n<cputune>\n<vcpupin vcpu='0' cpuset='0'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='1' cpuset='1'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='2' cpuset='4'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='3' cpuset='5'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='4' cpuset='8'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='5' cpuset='9'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='6' cpuset='12'/>\n<vcpupin vcpu='7' cpuset='13'/>\n</cputune>\n...\n</domain>\n[/code]\n\n(0, 1), (4, 5), (8, 9), and (12, 13) are in different CPU clusters.\n\n**Parent topic:** [Multi-core and NUMA](<kunpengprogramming_05_0002.html>)\n",
    "receipt": {
      "id": "fc7b069d-780f-4fec-99fa-7805f62bf31c",
      "runId": "d985a4dc-9025-4547-8001-b74e259ac960",

09　程序写版本，Agent 另外构造测试问题

通过版本找回正文，通过查询核对身份

complete 使用同一 sessionId；storage.version 定位版本目录及 manifest。检索用 query、sourceId、candidate/draft 等字段，三个命中章节都返回先前的 knowledgeId。manifest 以路径和哈希标记文件。
